<meta>This page continues the verse-by-verse commentary on the Book of Obadiah, specifically analyzing the geographic expansion of the restored kingdom. The previous page discussed the "House of Jacob" and "House of Joseph" as fire and flame; here, the focus shifts to how the faithful will inherit the lands of their former enemies and neighbors through the spread of the Gospel.</meta>

### EXAMINATION OF ABARBENEL

Repeating the verse, they explain: *“The Jews shall possess the south with the mountain of* **ESAU** *(or: toward the south, the mountain of Esau)”*—**THE MOUNTAIN OF ESAU**, meaning the neighboring Idumea <note>The region of Edom, located south of the Dead Sea</note>; the Christians, Apostles, and other disciples, who are to be in the south, on **Mount Zion** (namely, the place where the Gospel will first be preached), will bear fruit through their preaching among the Idumeans, according to Blessed Luther <note>Martin Luther (1483–1546), the primary figure of the Protestant Reformation</note>. **AND THE PLAIN** (referring to the inhabitants of the lowland, which was the second part of the tribe of Judah according to Joshua 15:33; the third part was called the hill country, in verse 48) shall possess the **PHILISTINES**, their neighbors (earlier and here, repeating the subject from elsewhere, they explain it as: *“The plain with the Philistines”*); and these same shall **POSSESS THE FIELDS OF EPHRAIM AND SAMARIA**: this was fulfilled through the Apostles when they went into Samaria to announce the Gospel, as Luther notes. Moreover, the **BENJAMINITES**, neighbors to the tribe of Judah, shall possess **GILEAD**. 

Worthy of note are the things Ribera <note>Francisco Ribera (1537–1591), a Spanish Jesuit theologian known for his biblical commentaries</note> says here in section 118: *“The children, who at first were few in Judea and enclosed within narrow borders, poured themselves out into all parts of the world, dividing cities and provinces, each beginning from those closest to him. So we see it happened at the beginning of the preaching of the Gospel”* in Acts 8: *“And they were all scattered throughout the regions of Judea and Samaria, except the Apostles.”* And soon after: *“Therefore, those who were scattered went about preaching the word of God.”* In the same place we read that the Apostles came to Samaria and that Philip was carried away to Palestine, that is, to Azotus. And in chapter 10, Peter was in Joppa and came to Caesarea to Cornelius the centurion. And in chapter 11, those who had been scattered came as far as Phoenicia, where Sarepta was, and Cyprus, and Antioch. 

It is also pleasing to add the words of **Gualther** <note>Rudolf Gualther (1519–1586), a prominent Swiss Reformed pastor</note> in sermon 4, page 123: *“After the return from Babylon, the Jews never extended the boundaries of their kingdom so far. But the Apostles, according to Christ’s command, began to preach in Zion and Jerusalem; then, when persecution arose there, as if with scattered forces, they occupied all of Judea, and soon entering Samaria, they brought this also to Christ. From there they invaded the regions of Sidon, and so great was the power of the Gospel preaching that it could not even be restrained by the sea, but rather penetrated the islands and all parts of the earth; and the Benjaminite Paul, within a few years, filled all places from Jerusalem as far as Illyricum <note>A Roman province in the western Balkans</note> with the Gospel.”* 

But also, through Christ, the one Shepherd and Lord, the scattered kingdom of Israel will be united again to the kingdom of Judah, for **AND THE EXILE**, meaning the ten exiled tribes, **OF THIS HOST** (In our text, the word <term>החיל</term> <note>Hebrew: *ha-chayil*, meaning "host," "army," or "strength"</note> seems to have the meaning of 'beginning.' Thus the Septuagint <note>The ancient Greek translation of the Old Testament</note> translates it: *“the beginning of the migration”* <note>original Greek: "της μετοικεσίας ή αρχή"</note>; likewise the Arabic: *“The beginning of the prey”* <note>original Arabic: "ראש אל סבא" / ra's al-saba</note>; and the Syriac: *“The first captivity”* <note>original Syriac: "שביתא קדמיתא" / shabita qadmayta</note>. Johannes Cocceius <note>17th-century Dutch theologian</note>, however, translates it as *“Of this wall.”* But since the meaning 'army' is both more famous and more convenient...

<summary>This page interprets the geographic restoration promised in Obadiah as a spiritual reality fulfilled by the Apostles. It argues that the expansion into territories like Samaria, Philistia, and Illyricum represents the spread of the Gospel throughout the known world rather than a literal military conquest.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the verse-by-verse commentary on the Book of Obadiah, specifically focusing on verse 20. It examines the geographical locations of the Jewish diaspora—Zarephath and Sepharad—and debates their identification with modern nations like France and Spain.</meta>

### ON OBADIAH.

Because of the great number of captives, it has pleased us—along with Blessed Luther, Vatablus, Mercerus, the Belgians, and many others—to read: *“OF THE CHILDREN OF ISRAEL”* (here the word *“shall possess”* must be repeated from the shared context <note>original: *ἀπὸ κοινοῦ* (apo koinou), a grammatical term for a word that applies to two clauses</note>) all *“WHICH”* belong to the *“CANANITES.”* (The Chaldean, the Rabbis, Pagninus, and Vatablus translate this as: *“who are among the Canaanites.”* Our author and Montanus <note>Benito Arias Montano (1527–1598), a Spanish orientalist and editor of the Antwerp Polyglot</note> use a rather harsh omission of words <note>term: *ellipsis* - a grammatical construction where words are left out</note>: *“As the Canaanites.”* Our author rightly rejects the feverish fiction of Aben Ezra and Kimchi <note>Abraham ibn Ezra and David Kimchi, influential medieval Jewish commentators</note>, followed by Arias Montanus, that the Germans are the Canaanites—for what could be more absurd to hear? Those who can transform Persians into Turks, Italians into Edomites, the people of Zarephath into Gauls <note>Modern-day French</note>, those of Sepharad into Spaniards, and Canaanites into Germans—what will they eventually make of themselves?) *“EVEN UNTO ZARPAT”*—that is, Zarephath of the Sidonians, mentioned in 1 Kings 17:9 and Luke 4:26. (Here our author, along with other Jews, understands *Zarpat* to mean France <note>original: *Galliam*</note> [but not Spain, which is a very unskillful oversight <note>original: *ἀβλεψία* (ablepsia)</note> by Calvin], and I know not what mountains of gold he promises to himself and his people. Lyranus <note>Nicholas of Lyra (c. 1270–1349), a Franciscan teacher</note>, led astray by them, refers this to St. Saminianus and St. James, the former of whom supposedly spread the word of **GOD** among the Senones in France, and the latter in Spain. However, Zarephath was a well-known town in the territory of the Canaanites, halfway between Tyre and Sidon, as Josephus <note>Flavius Josephus (c. 37–100 AD), the Romano-Jewish historian</note> says.) *“AND THE CAPTIVITY OF JERUSALEM”* (meaning the people of Jerusalem who were carried away toward Babylon) *“WHICH IS IN SEPHARAD.”* (Blessed Tarnovius, Junius, and Grotius, among others, take this as a common noun: *“In the boundary of dominion,”* as if derived from *Soph* and *Radad* <note>Hebrew roots: סוף (end) and רדד (to beat down/subdue)</note>; this would certainly be a most unusual way to compose a name in Hebrew, and no less an absurd guess <note>original: *στοχασμὸς* (stochasmos)</note> than that of the Jesuit Lapide, that *Sepharad* [ספרד] could come from *Soph* and *Radad*. Behold, the "liver of Crates"! <note>A Latin idiom, *Hem epar Cratetis*, referring to a confusing or messy heap of things</note> It would be more plausible to pull in the Chaldean *Sapir* [ספיר], meaning a shore or port, since Jerome clearly records that in the language of the Assyrians, the word signifies a "boundary." Meanwhile, according to our author and his kinsmen—whom Lyra, Castro, Cornelius à Lapide, and many other Papists follow—*Sepharad* is *Spain* <note>original: *Hispania*</note>, just as the Chaldean and Syrian versions translated this word; and the Pseudo-Gorionides calls Spain *Sepharad* [ספרד] more than once, as in Book 3, Chapter 15 and Book 5, Chapter 46. The Jesuits, who try to flatter the King of Spain, call for support on the grounds that the Greeks call Spain *Hesperida* or *Sperida*; but Ribera (section 106) has already cut the throat of this opinion and saved us the work. Furthermore, the Jew who told Jerome <note>St. Jerome (c. 347–420 AD), the translator of the Vulgate</note> that the place was called the Bosphorus, where Hadrian had led the Jews, was—as sometimes happens elsewhere—deceiving him. Thus it is ridiculous that some people insist on an allusion to the word *B-Sepharad* [בספרד], where the first letter is not part of the root; Mayer (in *Philologia Sacra*, part 1, page 196) rightly says that the Greeks never even dreamed of this when they spoke of their Bosphorus. Meanwhile, let Ribera, Sa, Mariana, Lapide, and others settle it among themselves whether they mean the Thracian, the Cimmerian, or the Gaditan Bosphorus.)

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the final verses of the Book of Obadiah. Having previously discussed the locations of the exiles, the author now addresses the identity of Sepharad and critiques Isaac Abarbenel’s interpretation of the final victory of Israel over Edom (Rome/Christians).</meta>

### EXAMINATION OF ABARBENEL ON OBADIAH.

as they wish. To us, Sepharad <note>original: *ספרד* (Sepharad), a biblical location traditionally associated by Jews with Spain, though the author here suggests Babylonia</note> seems to have been a place on the borders of Babylonia, to which the people of Jerusalem had been led away. For the story told by our author concerning Pyrrhus <note>A legendary reference to a Greek figure supposedly assisting in the exile of Jews to the West</note> is a fabrication; nor does the saying "He himself said it" <note>original: *Αὐτὸς ἔφα* (Autos epha), a Greek phrase used to denote an appeal to authority rather than reason</note> hold any weight here. Let him likewise take care lest, while he twists himself to find Jews in Spain and France, he meanwhile loses his own people in Italy—the Idumaeans <note>The descendants of Esau; in medieval Jewish commentary, Edom or Idumaea became a coded term for Rome and the Christian Church</note>. THE CITIES OF THE SOUTH refer to the Jewish cities in the southern part of the promised land. However, the Prophet here further exaggerates the expansion of the Gospel, which also penetrates throughout all of Canaan and Babylonia, of which we have already spoken: *“And as for his naming the exiles of Jerusalem specifically, I think it was done for this reason: because the Gospel was to be propagated from Jerusalem to the ends of the earth. But first in Jerusalem and Judea, that is, in the cities of the south, it was to be proclaimed especially by those from Jerusalem.”* I think this is what Christ said to the disciples in Acts 1: *“You shall be witnesses to me not only in Jerusalem, but also in all Judea and Samaria, and even to the ends of the earth.”* Samaria is the region of the ten tribes. And that some of the ten tribes were among the Apostles or their disciples is certain according to the flesh, for they were then scattered throughout the world, as Mercerus <note>Jean Mercier (c. 1510–1570), a prominent French Hebraist</note> notably remarks. AND SAVIORS SHALL ASCEND (The Dutch first apply this to Christ himself and add the reason: *“Thus the plural number was taken as something great and special.”* <note>original: *Also wardt het veelvuldig getal so in t genomen vor yet grootes ende besonders*</note> See Job 14:6, 20; Psalm 73:22; Proverbs 30:2). Rather, the plural word suggests the Apostles gathered in Jerusalem, who are called "Saviors," not in a creative or masterly sense <note>original: *ποιητικῶς & κυριακῶς* (poiētikōs & kyriakōs)</note>, but in an instrumental and ministerial sense <note>original: *ὀργανικῶς & διακονικῶς* (organikōs & diakonikōs)</note>, since they are ministers of salvation through the Gospel: 1 Timothy 4:16; James 5:20; Jude v. 23; 1 Corinthians 6:22, etc. AND THEY SHALL JUDGE, they shall convict them for their prior sins (Job 16:8), and they shall judge the twelve tribes of Israel (Matthew 19) THE MOUNT OF ESAU and any ungodly people like them, AND THE KINGDOM SHALL BE THE LORD’S; the head and supreme Monarch in that spiritual kingdom shall be Christ, the LORD our Righteousness <note>original: *יהוה צדקנו* (Yahweh Tzidkenu)</note> (Psalm 2:6, 8; Luke 1:33). Thus, in the genuine sense, Abarbenel’s interpretations easily vanish and become "sport for the winds." He is quite ridiculous among other things when he concludes that we Christians will one day be conquered by the Jews from the fact that Jacob feigned he would visit Esau; and toward the end, he especially betrays his fierce spirit, while he grants survival to all other nations but allows none at all to us. Thus also in Zechariah p. 294, c. 4, he says: *“The slaughter of the Turks in the future battle will not be as great as that of the Christians, for many of the Turks will escape according to Isaiah 66:19; but of the Christians, Obadiah said: 'There shall not be [a remnant],' etc.”* And on Isaiah f. 94. 4: *“All four kingdoms shall have remnants and a name, except the nation of Esau.”* And this is what I said: the vengeance of GOD upon this nation will be greater than upon the others, nor will said nation be held worthy of those things which the other peoples are. See also in Zephaniah 276, 1. Should our Devil <note>The author uses "Devil" here to refer to Abarbenel or the Jewish spirit of the commentary</note> be of a more hostile mind toward us, like an asp in the courtyard <note>original: *ἀσπίδ’ ἀυλίδιν* (aspid' aylidin)</note>? And yet some Christians tenderly nourish these "brood of vipers" <note>original: *γεννήματα ἐχιδνῶν* (gennēmata echidnōn)</note> in their bosom and prepare defenses for them. May Jesus Christ illuminate the minds of these blinded men—

->so that they may reflect on where they have fallen!<-

<meta>This page continues the critique of Isaac Abarbanel’s commentary on the Book of Obadiah. While the previous page provided an index of specific verses where Abarbanel allegedly corrupted the text, this page delves into the interpretive arguments regarding the location of Sepharad, the nature of the "Saviors" mentioned in verse 21, and the theological conflict over the identity of "Esau."</meta>

# EXAMINATION OF ABARBANEL ON OBADIAH.

...as they wish. To us, it seems that *Sepharad* was a place on the borders of Babylonia, to which the Jerusalemites had been led away. <note>The author is disputing the common identification of "Sepharad" with Spain (Iberia), arguing instead for a location near Babylon.</note> Indeed, our author’s history regarding Pythagoras is a fabrication; nor does the "he himself said it" <note>original: *αὐτὸς ἔφα*, a Greek phrase famously used by the followers of Pythagoras to denote the absolute authority of the master.</note> hold any weight here. Let him see to it that, while he twists himself to find Jews in Spain and France, he does not meanwhile lose his own people in Idumean Italy. <note>In medieval Jewish thought, "Edom" or "Idumea" was often used as a coded reference for Rome and, by extension, the Christian world.</note>
*THE CITIES OF THE SOUTH, namely the Jewish cities in the southern part of the Promised Land. Here the Prophet further examines the expansion of the Gospel, penetrating also into all of Canaan and Babylonia, of which we have already spoken. And as for his specifically naming the Jerusalemite exiles, I believe it was done because the Gospel was to be propagated from Jerusalem to the ends of the earth. But it was first promulgated in Jerusalem and Judea, that is, in the cities of the south, especially by the Jerusalemites. I believe this is what Christ said to the disciples in Acts 1: "You shall be my witnesses not only in Jerusalem, but also in all Judea and Samaria, and to the ends of the earth." Samaria is the region of the ten tribes. And from the Apostles or their disciples, some of the ten tribes, who were then scattered throughout the world, were reached according to the flesh,* as *Mercer* <note>Jean Mercier (Mercerus), a 16th-century French Hebraist.</note> notably says. AND SAVIORS SHALL ASCEND. (The Dutch first apply this to Christ himself and add the reason: *“Thus the multiple number was taken for the great and special one.”* <note>original Dutch: "Also wardt het veelvuldig getal so ingenomen vor net groote ende besondert."</note> See Job 14:6, 20; Psalm 73:22; Proverbs.)
Rather, the plural word suggests the Apostles gathered in Jerusalem, who are called "Saviors," not *creatively and authoritatively* <note>original: *ποιητικῶς & κυριακῶς*</note>, but *instrumentally and ministerially* <note>original: *ὀργανικῶς & διακονικῶς*</note>, since they are ministers of salvation through the Gospel: 1 Timothy 4:16; James 5:20; Jude 6:13; 1 Corinthians 6:22; etc. AND THEY SHALL JUDGE, they shall rebuke because of prior sins (John 16:8), and they shall judge the twelve tribes of Israel (Matthew 19) THE MOUNT OF ESAU and any impious people similar to them. AND THE KINGDOM SHALL BE THE LORD’S; the head and supreme Mountain in that spiritual kingdom shall be Christ, *The Lord Our Righteousness* <note>original Hebrew: יהוה צדקנו (*Yahweh Tsidkenu*)</note> (Psalm 2:6, 8; Luke 1:33). Thus, if taken in the genuine sense, Abarbanel's fancies easily vanish and become the mockery of the wind. Moreover, he ridiculously argues, among other things, that because Jacob feigned that he would visit Esau, we Christians will one day be defeated by the Jews. And near the end, he especially betrays his serpentine soul, while he grants a remnant and a name to all other nations but concedes none at all to us. *So also on Zechariah p. 294, c. 4, he says:*
*The slaughter of the Turks in the future battle will not be as great as that of the Christians, for many of them will escape according to Isaiah 66:19; but of the Christians Obadiah said: There shall not be [a remnant], etc. Isaiah f. 94. 4. For all four kingdoms there will be a remnant and a name, except for the nation of Edom [the Christians]. And this is what I said, that the vengeance of GOD upon this nation will be greater than upon the others, nor will said nation be held worthy of those things which other peoples are.* See also on Zephaniah 276: 1. Why would our Devil <note>A harsh polemical reference to Abarbanel.</note> be more <unclear>favorably disposed</unclear> to us? And yet some Christians tenderly nourish these *offspring of vipers* <note>original Greek: *γεννήματα ἐχιδνῶν*</note> in their bosom and prepare defenses for them. May Jesus Christ enlighten the minds of these blinded men, so that they may consider from where they have fallen!

->To God Alone be the Glory.<-

->// //<-
->My people, do your work:<-
<note>original Hebrew: "עמי עשי"</note>
->Greetings to the Kind Reader.<-
<note>original: "B. L. S.," an abbreviation for the Latin "Benevolo Lectori Salutem."</note>

We are undertaking a difficult task, one perhaps too heavy for our shoulders, as we strive to present for the mind's contemplation **Enoch**, the glory of the Patriarchs and the brightest star of the world before the Flood. For whether we weigh the gravity of the subject matter, or turn our attention to the intricate disputes of certain men—by which most things have been so confused that you might see them, according to that saying of the greatest Poet:

->All things lie uphill, downhill, across, and slanting.<-
<note>original Greek: "Πάντα γ’ ἄναντα κάταντα πάραντα καὶ δόχμια κεῖσϑαι," a line from Homer's Iliad (23.116) describing rough and difficult terrain.</note>

Or whether, finally, we look into ourselves and explore our own strengths, more arguments present themselves for declining or handing over this duty than for keeping it—a task more difficult to maintain than to perform. Indeed, the Divine Hero **Luther** seems to impose a perpetual silence upon us whenever we read his words, full of modesty, which he placed at the beginning of his golden *Commentaries on Genesis*:

> I am not the one, says that most blessed soul, of whom it can be said: **"I have done it."** Nor am I the one of whom you could say: **"He was doing it."** I stand in the last rank, one who hardly dares to say: **"I wished to do it."** And would that I were worthy to be the last in this final rank.

Let those "Thrasos" <note>A "Thraso" is a <term>stock character</term> from Roman comedy representing a "braggart soldier"—here used to describe arrogant young scholars.</note> listen to this, especially those youths of premature wisdom—for the black earth nourishes many devious men <note>original Greek: "διὰ τε πολλὺς βόσκει γαῖα μέλαινα πολυστρεφέας ἀνϑρώπες"</note>—who so easily persuade themselves that no one knows what they themselves do not know. This was once the funeral lament of *Bar Kippuki at the death of Rabbena*:

> If the flame has seized the cedars, what will become of the hyssop growing from the wall? If the Leviathan is caught by a hook, what will the fish in the marshes do? If the hook has fallen into a rushing torrent, what will the waters of the ponds do?

And the wise shall understand. <note>original Hebrew: "וחכם יבין"</note> We, truly, acknowledge our human limitation. <note>original Greek: "τὸ ἀνϑρώπινον"</note> In the meantime, when we look back at the footprints of so many Great Men who, it is established, walked rightly, we take heart and briskly move our feet forward—not to be leaders to others (for such arrogance <note>original Greek: "αὐϑάδεια"</note> has never come upon us) but to show whom they should follow.

<summary>In this preface, the author expresses the daunting nature of writing about the Patriarch Enoch, citing his own limitations and the humble example of Martin Luther. He warns against scholarly arrogance, using both Greek classical references and a Hebrew funeral parable to emphasize the difficulty of the task.</summary>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous preface, moving from a humble introduction into the formal analysis of the figure of Enoch. The author transitions from justifying his study to a systematic breakdown of Enoch’s names across different traditions (Jewish, Islamic, and Classical).</meta>

We are now about to set forth, in four chapters, most of the controversies concerning the history of Enoch (Genesis 5:22, 23, 24) that have been debated among philologists <note>A philologist in this period was a scholar of historical texts and languages.</note> until now, referring other matters to their own schools, where we profess our name not among the masters, but among the disciples. Furthermore, we shall everywhere pursue depth of thought rather than many words <note>original Greek: πολύνοιαν μᾶλλον ἢ πολυλογίαν</note>. We shall attack no one. If we find that someone has erred slightly, we will grant the same pardon that we ourselves hope for in return. We are but men, a thing prone to slipping into error <note>original Greek: ὀλίσθον πρὸς ἁμαρτίαν πρᾶγμα</note>. Perhaps I have fallen; he who went before me has fallen; and he who follows us will fall. Let us bear with one another. The praise belongs to the public. But with God present, every difficult thing becomes possible <note>original Greek: Θεο͂υ δὲ παρόντος ἅπαν ἄπορον πόριμον</note>.

## *CHAPTER ONE.*

# CONCERNING THE VARIOUS NAMES OF ENOCH.

*1. The name Chanoch: how was it changed into Henoch and Enoch? 2. Philo is noted for explaining Henoch as "gracious" <note>original Greek: κεχαρισμένον</note>. How is the verb "chanak" <note>original Hebrew: חנך</note> used regarding people and things? Enoch as if consecrated to God. Why is the other Enoch, the son of Cain, called by this name? A departure from the views of Bochart. 3. Are Annakos, Kannakos, or Nannakos actually Enoch? 4. To the Jews, Enoch is Metatron. This is otherwise the name of the "Angel of the Face." 5. Do the Jews say Enoch was an incarnate angel? Or do they believe he was changed into an angel? 6. From where is the name Metatron derived? Various opinions. A departure from the view of L. de Dieu. A word of Latin origin. 7. Edris in the Quran is not Noah, nor Ezra, but Enoch. This is proven. 8. It is common for Muhammad to alter the names of persons or substitute others. Various examples. 9. Why is Enoch called Edris? Notes on Drusius and Bochart. Enoch is the Atlas of the gentiles. Edris properly signifies a "teacher." 10. Enoch as a "Candidate for eternity," etc.*

->§. I.<-

The PATRIARCH whom we intend to discuss is called *Chanoch* <note>original Hebrew: חנך</note> in Scripture (*Genesis 5:18 and following*), a name we see changed by the Greeks and Latins into *Henoch* or *Enoch*. Specifically, the strong or double Hebrew aspiration of the letter *Chet* <note>The Hebrew letter ח</note> was turned into a Greek breathing mark, and was eventually regarded as superfluous; furthermore, the *Sheva* <note>A Hebrew vowel mark indicating a very short "e" or no vowel at all</note>, which is pronounced in various ways elsewhere, is here expressed as an *e*. From ex-

<meta>catchword: amples (originally: empla)</meta>

<summary>This page serves as a transition into a systematic study of Enoch, beginning with a table of contents for Chapter One. The author outlines the linguistic variations of Enoch's name across Hebrew, Greek, Latin, and Arabic (Quranic) sources, as well as his identification with the angel Metatron.</summary>
<keywords>Enoch, Chanoch, Etymology, Metatron, Edris, Quranic studies, Philology, Biblical Patriarchs, Bochart, Philo of Alexandria</keywords>

--- Page 118 ---
<meta>This page continues from the previous page's introduction to Chapter One, which outlines the study of Enoch's various names across different cultures. The author moves from the theoretical framework into a specific linguistic analysis of the name "Enoch" (Chanoch) and its roots in Hebrew and classical mythology.</meta>

To heap up similar examples in a matter known even to children would be the mark of a man wasting his leisure time.

§. 2. This name was given by his parents, Jared and his wife, in the universal language before the flood: **Hebrew**. In this regard, Philo Judaeus <note>A Jewish philosopher from Alexandria who sought to harmonize Greek philosophy with Jewish scripture.</note> in *On the Confusion of Tongues* (p. 264) and *On Abraham* (p. 276) interpreted "Henoch" as **one who has been gifted** <note>original Greek: κεχαρισμένον</note> or **your grace** <note>original Greek: χάριν σου</note>, as if the name were derived from the root *Hanan* <note>original Hebrew: חנן, meaning "to be gracious"</note>. In doing so, he revealed himself to be more learned in Greek than in Hebrew. Its genuine etymology, *Hanakh* <note>original Hebrew: חנך</note>, signifies **to initiate**. Primarily, it refers to a person: **to consecrate someone and to give them over to be trained for a certain kind of work or ministry, in which they are to remain for their entire life (Proverbs 22:6)**. Thus, Abraham in Genesis 14:14 is said to have armed his **trained servants** <note>original Hebrew: את חניכיו, referring to "novice recruits"</note> whom he had instructed, having destined them for various ministries and arts. Secondarily, the term denotes the **initiation of things**, such as a city, a temple, an altar, or a house, which was once customarily performed with solemn rites and joyful feasting. Therefore, Jared and his wife, out of holy devotion, desired through this naming—acting as a kind of vow, similar to that of **Hannah in 1 Samuel 1:11**—that their son Enoch be dedicated and consecrated to divine worship and ministry for his entire life. However, regarding the other **Enoch** who shares the name, the son of Cain, the illustrious Samuel Bochart <note>A 17th-century French Protestant scholar and polymath known for his work on biblical geography.</note> (*Sacred Geography, Part 1, Phaleg, book 1, chapter 15, p. 58*) believes he was called this because he was born during the solemn dedication of the city founded by his parent (Genesis 4:17). Thus, the son would have his name from the city, even though Scripture records the city was named after the son. Yet, it is possible that out of a similarly pious affection, at least from the mother, that name could have been given long before the city was founded.

§. 3. Furthermore, it might seem probable that the name of Enoch was known even to foreigners. This is suggested by the fact that Stephanus <note>Stephanus of Byzantium, a 6th-century author of a geographical dictionary.</note> mentions a certain **Annakos** in the entry for *Iconium* (to whom Iconium belonged, who lived over three hundred years, and regarding whom an oracle was issued stating that when he died, all would perish, etc.). Hermogenes, in his writings on the **Phrygians**, mentions **Kannakos** (a certain king of the Phrygians who foresaw and predicted the flood). Finally, Suidas <note>The author of the Suda, a massive 10th-century Byzantine encyclopedia.</note> mentions **Nannakos** (a predecessor of Deucalion <note>The "Greek Noah" who survived a great flood.</note>, who likewise foresaw the flood of Deucalion). These, however, are mere conjectures and a reaching for similar-sounding names. Another might say that **Kannakos** is perhaps **Cainan**, and **Nannakos** is **Noah**. Certainly, it cannot be asserted with certainty that these authors intended to refer to Enoch by the names Annakos, Kannakos, and Nannakos, even though Bochart (*Phaleg, book 2, chapter 13*) does not doubt it.

<summary>The author analyzes the Hebrew etymology of "Enoch" (meaning "to initiate" or "consecrate") and disputes Philo Judaeus's Greek-influenced interpretation. He also explores potential parallels to Enoch in Phrygian and Greek mythology through similar-sounding names like Annakos and Nannakos.</summary>

<keywords>Enoch, Etymology, Hebrew, Philo Judaeus, Samuel Bochart, Consecration, Annakos, Phrygia, Deucalion, Linguistics</keywords>

<meta>catchword: §. 5. Num. This page continues the discussion of Enoch’s life and identity by moving from the etymology of his proper name to the mystical titles and epithets attributed to him in Jewish tradition, specifically identifying him with the figure of Metatron.</meta>

§. 4. From the proper name **Enoch**, we now move to his **Epithets and honorary titles**. At the very beginning, the name **Metatron** <note>original Hebrew: מטטרון</note> presents itself, which is attributed to him in the historical records of the Jews. This is stated expressly in the **Targum** <note>An ancient Aramaic translation or paraphrase of the Hebrew Bible</note> that is commonly attributed to Jonathan ben Uzziel: *And Enoch served the Lord in truth, and behold, he was no more among the inhabitants of the earth, for he was snatched away and ascended into heaven by the word of the Lord; and his name was called Metatron, the great scribe.* <note>original Aramaic: ופלח חנוך בקושטא קדם יי והא ליתוהי עם דיירי ארעא ארום אתנגיד וסליק לרקיעא במימר קדם יי וקרא שמיה מטטרון ספרא רבא</note> The same may be found in the **Midrashim** <note>Rabbinic commentaries on scripture</note> and in the **Kabbalistic** <note>Jewish mystical and esoteric</note> books. *In the Kabbalistic books*, says Rabbi Abraham Zacuto in the *Juchasin* <note>A famous 16th-century Jewish history book titled "The Book of Lineage"</note> on page 5, *you will find that Enoch, the son of Jared, is Metatron; and the Blessed Name [God]—the first and the last, and there is no God besides Him—bore witness to this.* So too says David Cohen de Lara in his *Crown of the Priesthood* <note>original Hebrew: כתר כהונה, *Keter Kehunah*</note>, folio 54, column 2, line 50, citing the *Yalkut* <note>A collection of ancient rabbinic interpretations</note>: *Enoch, son of Jared, is Metatron.* It is, however, disputed in the **Tosafot** <note>medieval additions or glosses to the Talmud</note> on the **Talmudic Tractates** *Chullin* (folio 60) and *Yevamot* (folio 16, column 2, near the bottom of the page), whether Metatron is indeed Enoch, or rather an angel who is called the **Prince of the World** <note>original Hebrew: שר העולם, *Sar Ha’Olam*</note>. In the commentary on *Yevamot* at the place cited, it is said among other things:

> *And it is not possible that Enoch should be the Prince of the World, for in the chapter "These are the Trephot," it is said that during the six days of creation <term>Hexaemeron<gloss>the six days of the creation of the world</gloss></term>, the Prince of the World began to speak and said: "Let the Lord rejoice in His works." But Enoch did not yet exist during the six days of creation.* <note>original Hebrew/Aramaic: וא''א שיהיה חנוך שר העולם דבפ' אלו טרפות אמ' דבשעה ומי בראשית פתח שר העולם ואמר ישמח ה' במעשיו וחנוך לא היה במעשה בראשית</note>

However, it is shortly thereafter added: *it could be said that Metatron is not that same Prince of the World, etc.* <note>original Hebrew: וי''ל דמטטרון אין זה שר העולם</note> But regarding the angel Metatron—who is elsewhere called the **Prince of the Face** <note>original Hebrew: שר הפנים, *Sar HaPanim*, also known as the Angel of the Presence</note> because, as Elijah says in the *Tubi* (folio 53, 1), *he perpetually sees the face of the Supreme King*; likewise called the **Redeeming Angel**, the **Angel of the Covenant**, the **Guardian of Israel**, etc., by the Hebrews—there is no time now, nor does the nature of this present project allow me to add more. This is especially true since everything that could be said on this matter has already been stated by the most learned **Buxtorf the Younger** <note>A prominent 17th-century Christian Hebraist</note> in his *Exercises on the Ark of the Covenant* (chapter 14, page 131, and following). Meanwhile, we acknowledge along with him that the qualities everywhere attributed to this **Metatron** by the Hebrews are more excellent than those which would fit a merely created Angel.

<meta>This page continues the investigation into Enoch’s identity, moving from his Hebrew name to his mystical transformation into the angel Metatron. It addresses whether Enoch was always an angel or became one, and examines the linguistic origins of the name Metatron from Rabbinic, Greek, and Aramaic perspectives.</meta>

<sig>A 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: câ fa-</meta>

§. 5. Indeed, to observe this as if in passing <note>original Greek: ὡς ἐν παρόδῳ</note>, can it perhaps be drawn from what has been said that Cornelius à Lapide <note>A famous 17th-century Jesuit Bible commentator</note> writes in this place: that the Jews consider Enoch to be an incarnate angel? To our knowledge, they do not say this explicitly. You could more easily prove from their writings that Enoch turned into an angel upon his departure from this life. Certainly, the words of Rabbi Moses Albilda in *Olat Tamid* <note>The Perpetual Burnt Offering</note> (a philosophical commentary on the Pentateuch, folio 46, column 3) seem to point to this:

*It is possible for one who inherits his legacy to praise Enoch, because due to his great perfection, he saw his world [the world of souls] while still alive, and therefore death had no power over him. This is what is meant by: "And Enoch walked with God and he was not," meaning he did not live like every other man,* (the word "and he was not" <note>Hebrew: ואיננו, *ve-einennu*</note> can be interpreted by some in a negative sense) *because he left the status of a man and turned back into an angel.* <note>original Hebrew: וכבר אפשר ליורשו לשבחו של חנוך כי לרוב שלמותו ראה עולמו בחייו ולכן לא שלטה בו המיתה וז"ש/ ויתהלך חנוך את האלהים ואיננו ר"ל שלא חיה ככל אדם, (vox ואיננו Potest nonnulli in sinistram accipiunt partem) כי יצא ממדרגת איש ושב מלאך</note>

The sense is that he was no longer Enoch, since having exited the human condition, he returned as an angel. The *Targum of Pseudo-Jonathan* cited earlier certainly aims at the same point, so we may now pass over other examples.

§. 6. Before we move on from here, however, it seems necessary to explain what that much-celebrated name **Metatron** <note>Hebrew: מטטרון</note> properly signifies, since we have mentioned that Enoch is called Metatron. The Jews go in different directions on this. Some flee to the **Kabbalah** <note>The Jewish mystical tradition</note>; and since elsewhere the "Prince of the Face" <note>An angelic title for one who stands in God's immediate presence</note> is said to bear the name of **his Lord**, they note that here **Metatron** and **Shaddai** <note>Almighty; Hebrew: שדי</note> amount to the same number, namely 314 <note>This refers to Gematria, a system where Hebrew letters have numerical values; both names sum to 314</note>. Rabbi Bechai, in his commentary on *Exodus, folio 98*, advises that it can signify three things: 1. **Lord**, because the Rabbis call a "Lady" a *Matrona* <note>Hebrew: מטרונא</note>. 2. **Ambassador**, which the Greeks call *Menstor* <note>Hebrew: מנסטור; Johannes Buxtorf rightly conjectures in his *Talmudic Lexicon*, column 1192, that this refers to the Greek *metor* or leader</note>. Finally, 3. **Guardian**, because the Chaldeans <note>speakers of Aramaic</note> call a "Guard" *Matterat* <note>Hebrew: מטרה</note>. Rabbi Elias Levita, in the *Tisbi* <note>A famous 16th-century dictionary of difficult Hebrew and Aramaic words</note> at the place cited, says: *I heard from the Cardinal, my pupil,* <note>Referring to Cardinal Egidio da Viterbo, who studied under Levita</note> *that Metator in the Greek language denotes an "Ambassador," and perhaps that is so.* David de Pomis similarly says *he heard* that *Metator* in the Greek tongue has the value of a **Guardian** <note>Hebrew: שומר, *shomer*</note>. And yet, as far as is known, *Metator* in the Greek language signifies neither the one nor the other. The celebrated Ludovicus de Dieu, in his *Notes on Genesis 5:24*, thinks that *Metatron* is a corruption of *Mestor*, making it the Greek *metor* <note>original Greek: μήτωρ</note>, which according to Hesychius <note>An ancient Greek lexicographer</note> means a "leader" <note>original Greek: βγαλτηρ, *bgaltēr*</note>; this title would be especially fitting for a Heavenly Chancellor or **Great Scribe**. Certainly, in the Hebrew scriptures...

<sig>§. 5. Nun</sig>

§. 4. From the proper name of Enoch, we pass to his epithets and honorary titles. Here, at the beginning, the name **Metatron** <note>A high-ranking angel in Jewish mysticism often identified with the transformed Enoch</note> presents itself, which is attributed to him in the monuments of the Jews. Thus, it is stated explicitly in the Targum <note>An Aramaic translation or paraphrase of the Hebrew Bible</note> commonly ascribed to Jonathan ben Uzziel:

"And Enoch served the Lord in truth, and behold, he was no longer among the inhabitants of the earth, because he was taken away and ascended into heaven by the word of the Lord, and his name was called Metatron, the great scribe." <note>original Aramaic: לחנוך בקושטא קדם יי והא ליתוהי עם דיירי ארעא ארום אתנגיד וסליק לרקיעא במימר קדם יי וקרא שמיה מיטטרון ספרא רבא</note>

The same can be found in the Midrashim <note>Ancient rabbinic commentaries</note> and Kabbalistic books. In the books of Kabbalah <note>original Hebrew: ספרי קבלה</note>, Rabbi Abraham Zacuto says in *Juchasin* <note>The "Book of Genealogies," a 15th-century historical work</note>, page 5: "You will find that Enoch, son of Jared, is Metatron, and it is testified that God (blessed be He) is the first and the last, and there is no God besides Him." So also says David Cohen de Lara in *The Crown of the Priesthood* <note>Hebrew: כתר כהונה, *Keter Kehunah*</note>, folio 54, column 2, line 5, citing the *Yalkut* <note>A collection of rabbinic lore</note>: "Enoch, son of Jared, is Metatron."

However, there is a dispute in the *Tosefot* <note>Additions or supplements to the Talmud</note> (Talmudic Additions) in the tracts *Chullin* (folio 60) and *Yevamot* (folio 16, column 2, near the end of the page): Is Metatron actually Enoch, or is he rather the angel called the **Prince of the World**? <note>original Hebrew: שר העולם, *Sar Ha-Olam*</note> In the commentary on *Yevamot* in the place cited, it is said, among other things:

"It is impossible for Enoch to be the Prince of the World, for in the chapter 'These are the Unclean' <note>A section of the tractate Chullin</note> it is said that during the six days of creation <note>original Latin: Hexaemero</note>, the Prince of the World began to speak and said, 'Let the Lord rejoice in His works.' But Enoch did not exist during the six days of creation."

Yet, it is immediately added: "It can be said that Metatron is not that same Prince of the World," etc. But regarding the angel Metatron—who is elsewhere called the **Prince of the Face** <note>original Hebrew: שר הפנים, *Sar Ha-Panim*, meaning the angel who stands in God's immediate presence</note> because, as Elijah says in the *Tishbi* <note>A dictionary of rabbinic Hebrew by Elijah Levita</note> (folio 53), "he continually sees the face of the Supreme King"—he is also called by the Hebrews the **Angel Redeemer**, the **Angel of the Covenant**, the **Guardian of Israel**, and so forth.

There is no time now, nor does the purpose of this present work allow us to add more, especially since everything that could be said on this matter has already been stated by the most learned Johannes Buxtorf the Younger <note>A famous 17th-century Christian scholar of Hebrew and Jewish tradition</note> in his *Discourses on the Ark of the Covenant*, chapter 14, page 131 and following. In the meantime, we acknowledge with him that the qualities commonly attributed to this Metatron by the Hebrews are too exalted to belong to a merely created angel.

§. 5. Whether indeed—as we might observe in passing <note>original Greek: ὡς ἐν παρόδῳ</note>—it can perhaps be drawn from what has been said, as Cornelius à Lapide <note>A prominent 16th-17th century Jesuit biblical commentator</note> writes in this place, that the Jews consider Enoch to be an incarnate angel? As far as we know, they do not say this explicitly. You could more easily prove from their writings that Enoch turned into an angel upon his departure from this life. Certainly, the words of Rabbi Moses Albilda in *Olat Tamid* <note>"The Perpetual Burnt Offering," a philosophical commentary on the Torah</note>, folio 45, column 3, seem to point toward this: 

"It is possible (referring to the word *v’einenno* <note>"and he was not," from Genesis 5:24</note>, which some take in a negative sense) to explain this to the praise of Enoch: namely, that because of his extreme perfection, he saw his world (referring to the world of souls) while still alive, and thus death had no jurisdiction over him; hence it is said: *And Enoch walked with the Lord and he was not*, the sense being that he was no longer Enoch, since, having exited the human condition, he returned as an angel, etc." <note>original Hebrew: לרב וכבר אפשר ליורשו לשבחו של חנוך כי שלמותו ראתה עולמו בחייו ולכן לא צדק בו המות ויש ויתהלך חנוך את האלהים ואיננו ר"ל שלא היה חנוך כי יצא ממדרגת איש ושב מלאך</note> 

The previously cited Targum <note>Aramaic translation/paraphrase of the Bible</note> of Pseudo-Jonathan certainly aims at the same point, though we shall pass over other examples for now.

§. 6. Before we depart from this subject, however, it seems necessary to state what that oft-repeated name **Metatron** actually signifies, since we mentioned that Enoch is called Metatron. The Jews go in different directions on this. Some flee to the Kabbalah <note>The Jewish mystical tradition</note>; and since among other things the "Prince of the Face" <note>A high-ranking angel who stands in God's presence</note> is said to carry the name of his Lord, they note that the name *Metatron* and the divine name *Shaddai* <note>"Almighty"</note> result in the same numerical value, namely 314. <note>This is the practice of Gematria: Metatron (מטטרון: 40+9+9+200+6+50) and Shaddai (שדי: 300+4+10) both equal 314.</note> Rabbi Bechai, in his commentary on *Exodus*, folio 98, advises that it can signify three things: 

1. **Lord**, because "Lady" is called *Matrona* <note>מטרונא</note> by the Rabbis. 
2. **Legate** <note>A messenger or envoy</note>, which the Greeks call *metator* <note>מנטטור; Johannes Buxtorf rightly conjectures in his *Lexicon Chaldaicum, Talmudicum et Rabbinicum* that this refers to the Greek *mēnytōr* (μηνύτωρ), meaning an informer or messenger</note>. 
3. Finally, **Guardian**, because the Chaldeans <note>Aramaic speakers</note> call a "guard-post" or "custody" *matterat* <note>מטרה</note>. 

Rabbi Elias Levita, in *The Tishbite* <note>A famous 16th-century dictionary of Hebrew terms</note>, says: "I heard from the Cardinal, my student, that *metator* in the Greek language denotes a Legate, and perhaps it is so." <note>original Hebrew: שמעתי מהקרדינאל תלמידי כי מטטור בלשון יון שליח ואולי כן הוא. The student mentioned is likely Cardinal Egidio da Viterbo, who studied Hebrew under Levita.</note> David de Pomis similarly says he heard that *metator* in the Greek language is equivalent to a **Guardian**. And yet, *metator* in the Greek tongue, as far as is known, denotes neither the one nor the other. The celebrated Ludovicus de Dieu, in his *Observations on Genesis 5:24*, thinks that *Metatron* is a corruption of *mastiōr*, making it the Greek *mētor* <note>μήτωρ</note>, which according to Hesychius <note>A 5th-century Greek grammarian</note> means a "purse" or "pouch"; a title which would be quite fitting for a Celestial Chancellor or Great Scribe. Certainly in the Hebrew Scripture...

<meta>This page continues the philological investigation into the name "Metatron," specifically debating its linguistic origins. The previous page discussed Enoch’s transformation into an angel; this page shifts from Jewish rabbinic etymologies to Islamic traditions regarding the same figure, identified there as "Idris."</meta>

<sig>i.</sig>

since *Mastor* <note>original Hebrew: מסטור</note> could easily transition into *Metatron* <note>original Hebrew: מטטור</note> due to the similarity of the letters; but who would easily change the name *Metor* <note>original Greek: μήτωρ, meaning "mother"</note> written in Greek (from which, however, it is claimed the corruption must have happened) into *Metattos* <note>original Greek: μήττως</note> or *Metator*? In this, therefore, the Rabbis were mistaken: they hoped for an explanation of the name from the Greek language, when it was actually sought from a Latinism. For in Latin, a *Metator* <note>A Roman military term for a scout or officer who goes ahead to mark out a camp</note> is a "guide of the way" <note>original Hebrew: מורה דרך; original Greek: ὁ ὁδηגὸς</note>, as Rabbi Moses Gerundensis <note>Also known as Nahmanides (1194–1270), a leading medieval Jewish scholar</note> correctly explained regarding *Exodus* 23; or a "designator of lodgings," as Rabbi David Cohen de Lara explains in *City of David* <note>original Hebrew: עיר דוד</note>, *page 51*, as a *furier* <note>A quartermaster or harbinger who prepares housing</note>. The word even passed from the Latins to the Copts. Furthermore, the angel Metatron is said to have led the Israelites in the desert and to have defined their journeys and resting places.

§. 7. From the Jews, we proceed to the Muslims <note>original Latin: Muhammedanos</note>. Their Impostor <note>A common polemical term for Muhammad used by 17th-century Christian scholars</note> in his *Al-Koran* mentions *Edris* <note>Idris</note> more than once. Thus in the *Sura of Mary* <note>original Hebrew: מרים</note> *chapter 19, verses 55 and 56, page 555* in our copy (which we use through the kindness of the Reverend and Excellent Mr. Heinrich Philipp Herwart, our esteemed patron), he says: *And mention in the Book Idris; indeed, he was a man of truth, a prophet.* <note>Original Arabic (transliterated into Hebrew script in source): ודכר פי אלכתאב אדריס אנה כאן צדיקא נביא</note> *And We raised him to a high place.* <note>Original Arabic: ורפענאה מכאנא עליא</note> Likewise, in the *Sura of the Prophets* <note>original Hebrew: אלאנביא</note> *chapter 21, verse 86, page 589* in our copy: *And Ishmael and Idris and Dhul-Kifl (Elijah); all were among the patient.* <note>Original Arabic: ואסמעיל ואדריס ודאלכפל כל מן אלצאברין</note> However, as to which of the saints that deceiver <note>original Latin: Sycophanta</note> wished to indicate by that name *Edris*, scholars do not agree. Antonio Giggei, in the first volume of his *Arabic Thesaurus, page 61*, understands him to be Noah. Filippo Guadagnoli, in his *Apology for the Christian Religion, pages 7 and 40*, believes him to be Ezra, perhaps deceived by the Latin translator of the Koran by some similarity of sound; he was noted for this by the famous Hottinger in his *Key to Oriental Etymology*. But the most excellent philologists have long advised that he should be understood as Enoch. See Gabriel Sionita, *On the Religion and Customs of the Orient, page 54*; Abraham Ecchellensis, *History of the Arabs, page 262*; Wilhelm Schickard in his *Persian History, page 24*; Jacob Golius, *Arabic Lexicon, page 817*; John Selden, *On the Natural Law and the Law of Nations, book 5, chapter 9, page 609*; Louis de Dieu on *Genesis 5:24*; and Hottinger in his *Philological Treasury, page 85, Oriental History, page 21*, and *Oriental Miscellany, page 239*. Certainly, the Impostor always addresses Noah by his own name, *Nuh* <note>original Hebrew: נוח</note>, whence Sura 71 is titled *The Sura of Noah* <note>original Hebrew: סורת נוח</note>. Indeed, in the Muhammadan prayers published by the distinguished Henning Henningii, *page 172*, Idris is expressly distinguished from Noah. For the Muslim prays "by the truth of Idris"...

He then identifies the Quranic figure Idris as the biblical Enoch, refuting theories that Idris is Noah or Ezra by citing various 17th-century Orientalist scholars.</summary>

i. e. and his elevation, and by the truth of Noah and his ship <note>original Hebrew/Arabic: ורפעתה ובחק נוח וספינתה</note>: *By the truth of Idris and his ascension; by the truth of Noah and his ship.* Furthermore, the circumstances added in the Quran <note>original: Alkorano</note> fit Enoch perfectly. For he is placed alongside Elijah and is said to have been raised to a higher place. Finally, there are the clear testimonies of the Arabs themselves, produced by Selden and Hottinger in the works cited above, which there is no need to copy out here.

§. 8. Moreover, let no one wonder at the metamorphosis of the name *Enoch* into *Idris*. It was the custom of that most crafty Impostor <note>A common, though biased, 17th-century term for Muhammad</note> to intentionally alter and change the names of people in such a way, as the distinguished Hottinger—who is most eagle-eyed in these matters—shows above others, and to whom we therefore refer the reader. Thus, that Trifler calls Eber *Hud* <note>original Hebrew: הוד</note>. (*Oriental History, page 26, at the bottom.*) He calls Peleg *Zalech* <note>original Hebrew: צאלח; likely Salih</note>. (*p. 30.*) He calls Nimrod *Temud* <note>original Hebrew: תמוד; referring to the tribe of Thamud</note>. (*p. 31.*) Jethro he calls *Shoaib* <note>original Hebrew: שעיב</note> (*p. 53 f.*) Elijah he calls *Dilkefel* <note>original Hebrew: דולכפל; Dhul-Kifl</note> (*p. 39.*) Elisha he calls *Jasin* <note>original Hebrew: יאסין; Yasin</note>. (*p. 61.*) Jonah he calls *Dalnun* <note>original Hebrew: אלנון; Dhu’l-Nun</note>, that is, the fish-man <note>original Greek: ιχθυανθρω-</note> (*p. 40.*) Ezra he calls *Ofair* <note>original Hebrew: עזיר; Uzair</note> (*p. 62.*) Our Lord Jesus (blessed forever) he calls *Isa* <note>original Arabic: עיסי</note> (*p. 102.*) John he calls *Yahya* <note>original Arabic: יחיי</note> (*p. 96.*) You may also see the Deceiver sometimes playing ineptly and childishly with rhyming endings <note>original Greek: ὁμοιοπτώτῳ, referring to words with similar case endings or sounds</note>. Abel and Cain are to him *Abel* and *Kabel* (*p. 19.*) Goliath and Saul are *Galut* and *Talut* (*p. 75.*) Gog and Magog are *Jagog* and *Magog*; the Angels of confusion in the Laws are *Harut* and *Marut*, etc. We do not wish to dwell on these absurdities any longer.

§. 9. But you might ask: What is the reason for this? What, after all, does Enoch have to do with Idris? The author of the book *Juchasin* <note>The "Sefer Yuchasin" or Book of Genealogies by Abraham Zacuto</note> wrote on *folio 134*: *Enoch, who is called Idris* <note>original Hebrew: חנוך אשר נקרא אדריס</note>. At the sight of this word, Drusius <note>Johannes Drusius (1550–1616), a Protestant Old Testament scholar</note> was as terrified as one who has stepped on a snake with bare heels. He admits in his book on Enoch, *page 23*, that this reading is "smoke in his eyes" <note>An idiom meaning it is confusing or obscure</note>. *Should it*, he asks, *be read Adlis, or Atlis, that is, Atlas? For it is reported in Eusebius, from the ancient writer Eupolemus, that the Atlas of the gentiles was actually Enoch.* When Bochart <note>Samuel Bochart (1599–1667), a French biblical scholar</note> produced this passage of Eusebius in *Phaleg, page 101*, he added: *In these words it should be noted that Enoch and Atlas are considered the same person. Was it from this fact—that just as Atlas is called Duris and Dyris by the Phoenicians—that Enoch is called Idris by the Arabs?* But the learned men have long since dispelled the "smoke" of Drusius. Bochart's conjecture, by which he contends for the Punic *Dyris* with the Muhammadan *Edris*, proves very little. For it is not certain either that Muhammad drew "Edris" from the Phoenicians or that he knew anything at all about Atlas. The Arabs themselves remind us that Enoch is called *Idris* because of his frequent study <note>original Arabic script: לכתרה דרסה</note>. Indeed, among them, the root *darasa* <note>to study, learn, or read</note> is among other

## CHAPTER TWO.
# CONCERNING THE DURATION OF ENOCH ON EARTH.

1. Enoch is said to "walk with God." Rabbi Eliezer applies this to the <term>Embolism<note>The practice of adding an extra month to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year; Eliezer suggests Enoch discovered this calculation.</note></term>. 2. To others, it means being like an infant. To some Jews, Enoch was a hypocrite. 3. J. Bolduc places monastic orders before the time of Moses. To him, Enoch was a <term>Minorite<note>A member of the Franciscan order; Bolduc is making a historical leap by claiming Enoch lived as a monk.</note></term>. For him, "walking with God" means becoming a monk. 4. *The Kuzari* <note>A famous work by the Jewish philosopher Judah Halevi</note> applies the phrase to a solitary life. 5. "Walking" is transferred to mean morals and duties. Why does Maimonides call such walking "incorporeal"? 6. The <term>Hitpael<note>A Hebrew verb form that typically indicates reflexive or repeated action; the author suggests Enoch's walking was a continuous habit.</note></term> signifies frequency. 7. "Walking with God" (<note>Hebrew: Eth ha-Elohim</note>) denotes either holiness of life or of office. Luther translates it as "Walking God." Others: "with God." 8. "With" is said to imply here either company, or following, or reverence for a presence, or approval. 9. These views are not in conflict. It is proved that the phrase expresses holiness of life. Related phrases and the opinions of interpreters. Praise for Luther's translation. 10. Why is it said twice: "He walked with God"? One instance implies his office, the other his piety. Enoch as Prophet and Priest. Regarding his slain brothers, according to <note>George Cedrenus, an 11th-century Byzantine historian</note> Cedrenus. 11. Was Enoch the inventor of Astronomy (specifically the Embolism) and Grammar? The "Enochian alphabet" of Pantheus, Duret, and Hepburn. The origin of letters. 12. Enoch's firstborn, Methuselah. Can Adam be said to have lived longer than him? The shameful error of Henriquez. 13. ->§. 1.<-

Having now set aside the matter of the name, we shall approach Enoch himself. We will not, however, pursue everything that could be said on this occasion, but primarily the controversies. Thus, we must briefly explain the course of Enoch's life and his manner of living with people on earth. Furthermore, the Divine historian [Moses] has embraced everything in barely three words: **And Enoch walked with God** (Genesis 5:22 and 24). Regarding the meaning and emphasis of this phrase, there is such disagreement among authors and so many various opinions are usually brought forward that the line from **Homer** might be used:

> What first, what next, what last shall I recount? <note>original Greek: Τί πρῶτον, τί δ' ἔπειτα, τί δ' ὑστάτιον καταλέξω;</note>

Let us see the more important ones, and first indeed the wonderful interpretation of **Rabbi Eliezer** in *Chapters*, chapter 8, whose words **Vorstius** thus translated (for it is not permitted to inspect the author’s original text at the moment): *Adam handed down to Enoch (namely, the calculation of the year, etc.) who, being introduced into the mystery of the <term>Embolism<note>The practice of adding an extra month to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year.</note></term>, intercalated the year, according to that saying: "And Enoch walked before God"—namely, Enoch walked in the ways of the world’s calculation, which God had handed down to Adam.* Indeed, what could be asked for that is more reasonable? What greater mystery could be revealed than that "to walk before God" means "to intercalate the year"? Who would have thought of the <term>Embolism</term> here, unless the legendary Eliezer had suggested it? Truly no one; *not even in a dream* <note>original Greek: οὐδ' ὡς ὄναρ</note>. I add nothing except the three words of **Julian** <note>The Emperor Julian "the Apostate," known for his pithy response to Christian writings.</note>: *I read it, I understood it, I condemned it.* <note>original Greek: ἔγνων, ἀνέγνων, κατέγνων</note>

->§. 2.<-

Another interpretation is given, no less absurd, if not extremely impious, declaring that "walking with God" means *to be like an infant*. It has Rabbis as its authors—here, certainly, most infantile ones. **Menasseh ben Israel** mentions it in his treatise *On Human Frailty*, page 67, and does not show himself to be very distant from it. He speaks, however, of Noah, to whom the same phrase is attributed in Genesis 6:9. "It is not irrelevant to the purpose," he says, "what some have noted, that the Scripture says: 'Noah walked *with* God,' but Abraham was addressed by God thus: 'Walk *before* me and be perfect.' From this they infer that Noah was like an infant who could not yet walk, so that he needed God to lead him by the hand; but Abraham, who was well-instructed in all things, was able to walk alone and secure, because he surpassed Noah by many <term>parasangs<note>A Persian unit of distance, approximately 3.5 miles; used here metaphorically to mean a great distance.</note></term>." He also continues; and with the planting...

Furthermore, there have been some among the *Jews* who felt no less harshly about *Enoch*. Certainly, *Rabbi Solomon Jarchi* <note>A famous 11th-century French rabbi, also known as Rashi, whose commentaries are central to Jewish study.</note> notes on these words "And Enoch walked" <note>original Hebrew: ויתהלך חנוך</note>: *that he was indeed righteous, but of an inconstant mind and easily turned back toward wickedness.* Indeed, *Rabbi Ibbo* in *Genesis Rabbah* <note>An ancient collection of Jewish homiletic interpretations of the Book of Genesis.</note> called him a hypocrite <note>original Hebrew: חנף, *chaneph*</note>. §. 3. Next to these let us bring forward *Jacques Bolduc of Paris*, a *Christian* indeed, but in his passion for inventing fables, he is easily second to none of the *Jews*. He, therefore, in his books concerning the Church before the Law, strives to show that the ecclesiastical order <note>original Greek: τάξιν, *taxin*</note> of the *Papists* exactly matches that of the ancient *Patriarchs*. To this foolish writer, *Noah, Shem, Eber,* and *Abraham* are <term>Archimandrites<note>In Eastern Christianity, the superior of a monastery or group of monasteries.</note></term>, who all lived in *Palestine* outside of cities, had their own monasteries, and were called *Rephaim*. To ensure no folly is missing, he even adds *Ham* and *Nimrod* to their number as if they were members of religious orders. He also claims that under the High *Pontiff* *Shem* (whom he identifies as *Melchizedek*), there were certain orders of religious men called *Nephilim* because they prostrated themselves in prayer; some of these were *Enochiates* (later called *Essenes*) who derived their contemplative life from *Enos* before the flood, like the *Carthusians*; others were *Cinaites* after *Cainan*, who adopted the "mixed life" of the *Friars Minor* <note>The Franciscans, a Catholic religious order.</note>. He even asserts that the knights of the Order of *Jerusalem* were established at that time.

Furthermore, in Book 1, Chapter 14, he makes *Enoch* a member of the order of *Cinaites*, claiming he advanced so much in that most devoted congregation over the span of 300 years that after many years he was frequently sent as an angel of God by *Enos*—who acted in God's name and stead—and also by his *great-great-grandfather Cainan*, to various regions where the monasteries and fellowships of the sons of God were scattered. He was sent to the brothers of both the *Enochiate* and *Cinaite* congregations so that, by his frequent preaching and example, he might rebuke their loose morals and lives that had become entirely foreign to the ancient way of living, and correct them by the authority granted to him by *Enos*, the supreme general-prefect of both orders. From this he explains the phrase "Walking with God" <note>original Greek: τὸ Ambulare cum Deo</note>: in those ancient times, that expression meant the same as solemnly and perpetually dedicating oneself entirely to divine service through certain ceremonies, and so some...

than to enter those religious societies. Certainly, it is certain that Enoch devoted himself entirely to divine service: but to say that walking with GOD is the same thing as entering some monastic order and almost becoming a Capuchin <note>A member of the Order of Friars Minor Capuchin, a reform movement within the Franciscans known for their austerity.</note>—this is as easily rejected as it is shamelessly and irrationally asserted by the Capuchin author.

§. 4. The opinion of *Rabbi Judah Halevi* <note>A celebrated 12th-century Jewish philosopher and poet.</note> seems somewhat related. He was a Jewish writer who was otherwise not without skill, but in his famous book *Kuzari* <note>A defense of Judaism written in Arabic, later translated into Hebrew and Latin; it takes the form of a dialogue between a king and a rabbi.</note> he leans toward the idea that the phrase *to walk with GOD* implies a solitary and eremitic <note>Living as a hermit or recluse.</note> life. His words in *Part 3, near the beginning* are these: <note>original Hebrew: אך הוא מתאוה וזה אלו היה מגיע למדרגת חנוך... ("But he desires and longs for this, if he were to reach the rank of Enoch...")</note> which the famous Buxtorf <note>Johannes Buxtorf the Younger, a prominent 17th-century scholar of Hebrew.</note> translates on page 153 as: *He desires and longs for this (namely, solitude and separation from the company of men) only if he should reach the rank of Enoch, of whom we read: "And Enoch walked before GOD" (Genesis 5:24), or the rank of Elijah, of blessed memory. Thus, by leading a solitary life, he is united with the company of angels, so that a solitary life and seclusion are not troublesome to him. For they (the Angels) are his companions; indeed, he hates the multitude and the crowd of people, because he is free from the care of the things of this lower world and full of the vision and contemplation of the Kingdom of Heaven, which does not require food and drink. For those who are so prepared, perfect solitude is suitable.* But *Judah Halevi* carves a solitary life out of this phrase in vain, since neither the word nor the name implies solitude, nor does conversation with God remove one from human society. Rightly did *Blessed Luther* <note>The German Reformer Martin Luther.</note> say in his commentary on *Genesis, folio 78. a.*: *To walk with GOD,* he says, *is not to flee into the desert or to hide in a corner, but to go forth according to one’s calling and to oppose the iniquity and malice of Satan and the World, etc.*

Therefore, we prepare ourselves for the investigation <note>original Greek: ἐξερεύνησιν, *exereunēsin*</note> of the true meaning. We presuppose as known, however, that the word *halakh* <note>original Hebrew: הלך, meaning "to walk" or "to go."</note> *to walk, to go*, refers to morals, the way of life, and likewise the duties of of- <note>The text ends mid-word; the catchword "nerum" indicates the next page begins with "fices" (completing *munerum*).</note>
